The natural pattern for this loop is to split the list into traversed and remaining portions, with p as the boundary:

```
dllseg_shape(l, head, ?, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, ?)
```

- RP_pre_loop: p = l, so `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` which is the same as `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`. p's prev = head.
- RP_iter_2: p = y_287 (l->next), and `data_at(&(l@pre->next), struct list*, y_287)`. So p = l->next, and p's prev = l.
- RP_iter_3: p = y_293, p's prev = y_287 (i.e., p->prev = y_287).

```

This would say:
- The segment from l to p (with p not included), where l->prev = head and last node's next = p
- Plus the remaining list from p with p->prev as its prev pointer

This is a common pattern for traversing doubly-linked lists.

> Shape predicate arguments use program variable names (`x`, `t`, `u`, `y`) and C field expressions (`t->prev`) directly.

- p != 0: This is guaranteed by loop condition (entering the body)
- But the invariant must hold at ALL loop check points, including loop exit (p = 0)

For the property `dllseg_shape(l, head, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`:
- If p = 0 (empty list or at loop exit): `dlistrep_shape(0, anything) = emp`, `dllseg_shape(l, head, 0->prev, 0)` = ??? This doesn't work because you can't dereference NULL.

Actually, when p = 0, the assertion `dlistrep_shape(0, prev)` = `emp` (by the base case of the definition). And `dllseg_shape(l, head, ???, 0)` where ??? is 0->prev... we can't dereference NULL.

So this formulation doesn't work at loop exit (when p = 0). The invariant must hold at ALL check points including exit.

At loop exit:
- p = 0 (loop terminated because p was NULL)
- The state is just the original list: `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` 
- No segmentation needed since we exited

So the invariant can't use `p->prev` when p might be 0. We need a different approach.

At the invariant point:
- If the loop body DID execute, p has been advanced to p->next
- The condition about to be checked: `p` (which could now be NULL)

- Start: invariant holds, p = some value
- Check condition: if p != 0, execute body
- Body: p = p->next
- Back to invariant check: does invariant hold?

If p becomes 0 in the body, the condition check fails and the loop exits. But the invariant still needs to hold at the invariant check point.

So at the invariant point, p could be any value (including 0), as long as:
- If p = 0, the loop doesn't enter (exit path) - the state should support exiting to postcondition
- If p != 0, the loop body should preserve the invariant

OK, so for p = 0, the invariant must still be meaningful. The simplest approach:

```
/*@ Inv Assert dlistrep_shape(l, head) */
```

This is clean and correct. The issue is that QCP drops p from scope.

Hmm, what if I need to include the local variable binding for p in the invariant? The `data_at` for p's stack location. But the SKILL.md says not to include local items.

In QCP, when you use a while loop with an invariant, the invariant REPLACES the current assertion. If the invariant doesn't mention p, p disappears from the assertion, and QCP can't evaluate `while (p)`.

But QCP should still have the local variable binding for p from the program execution... unless the invariant mechanism replaces the entire assertion.

Now I can see the issue clearly. The entailment goal is:

```
Goal (left side / assertion):
exists l_286_value p_287_value,
p_281_addr l_272_addr,
(&p == p_281_addr) && (&l == l_272_addr) &&
(p_287_value != 0) &&
data_at(p_281_addr, struct list*, p_287_value) *
data_at(l_272_addr, struct list*, l_286_value) *
dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)
|--
Goal (right side / needed):
exists p_287_value,
,
data_at(&(p_287_value->next), struct list*, v)
```

QCP is trying to prove that from the current assertion (which includes `dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)`), it can derive `data_at(&(p_287_value->next), struct list*, v)` for the memory read `p->next`.

The issue is:
1. The left side has `dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)` which is the full list from l
2. The right side needs `data_at(&(p_287_value->next), struct list*, v)` - permission to read p->next
3. QCP needs to unfold `dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)` to match p to a node in the list

But crucially, the left side has `(p_287_value != 0)` as a PROP (from the loop condition) and `data_at(p_281_addr, struct list*, p_287_value)` which tells us p's value.

But the left side does NOT have `dlistrep_shape(p_287_value, ...)` - it has `dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)`. QCP needs to unfold from l_286_value and traverse next pointers until it reaches p_287_value.

QCP's strategies might not be able to do this traversal automatically. The strategies are for unfolding predicates, but they might need a direct connection between p and the list.

So the solution is to include `dlistrep_shape(p, ...)` or a similar predicate in the invariant that directly links p to the list.

```

Actually, let me look at this from another angle. The crucial insight is:

For `p = p->next;`, QCP needs `data_at(&(p->next), struct list*, ...)`. This comes from unfolding `dlistrep_shape(p, ...)` (where p is the first arg). If we have `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` and need to get to p which is somewhere in the middle, strategies need to repeatedly unfold dlistrep_shape from the start until they find p.

This is a very general operation that might not be supported by the strategies. The strategies might only unfold at the head of the list, not somewhere in the middle.

So the solution is to either:
1. Have the invariant include `dlistrep_shape(p, ...)` directly (split the list at p)
2. Add `has_permission(&p)` which somehow helps QCP link p to the list

State when p is in the middle of the list:
- p points to some node
- The traversed part: from l to p, not including p = `dllseg_shape(l, head, p->prev, p)`
- The remaining part: from p = `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`

But `p->prev` is a field access. When p = 0, this is problematic.

The question is: can QCP handle `p->prev` in an invariant when p is the first argument of `dlistrep_shape`?

Actually, wait. In the definition:
```
dlistrep_shape(l, p) = (l == 0 && emp) || exists t, data_at(field_addr(l, next), struct list*, t) * data_at(field_addr(l, prev), struct list*, p) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)
```

The second parameter of `dlistrep_shape` is the prev pointer of the first node. So `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` means the list starting at p where p->prev = p->prev, which is a tautology.

QCP might evaluate `p->prev` as a pure expression (not a field access). But then when it tries to use this in the SEP, it might create a `data_at` for the prev field.

Let me check the earlier run with `dllseg_shape(l, head, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)`. The error was:

```

So QCP IS creating a `data_at` for `p->prev` and it conflicts. This means `p->prev` in the invariant is treated as a field read, creating a `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)`.

This is a fundamental issue - using `p->prev` in the invariant creates a `data_at` for that field, but `data_at` for the same field also exists inside the predicates.

The solution might be to NOT use `p->prev` but instead use a fresh variable name that QCP can quantify.

What if I express the permission differently? Like:

```
/*@ Inv Assert dlistrep_shape(l, head) * data_at(field_addr(p, next), struct list*, p->next) */
```

No, this is wrong - it creates a duplicate data_at for p->next.

OK, new idea. What if I express the invariant by splitting `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` into:
- `data_at(field_addr(l, next), struct list*, l->next)` - doesn't help because we don't know l->next
- This isn't a proper split

Let me look at this from yet another angle. The successful test without annotation uses `dlistrep_shape(l, 0) * has_permission(&p)`. If `has_permission` can't be used with annotations, what would be the equivalent?

In QCP, `has_permission(&p)` gives permission to access the stack variable p. It keeps p in the scope of the assertion. Without it, p falls out of scope when the invariant is processed.

But wait, in Test 2 (with annotation and `p == p`), p stayed in scope! The error was about Memory Read, not about p being out of scope. So `p == p` successfully kept p in the assertion.

The issue is that even though p is in scope, QCP can't prove that `p->next` is safe. It needs to link p to the list predicate.

What if I add a PROP that says `p` is in the list? Like: `p == l`? No, this isn't always true.

Or `dlistrep_shape(l, head) && (p == l || p == l->next || ... )`? We can't enumerate.

Hmm, what if the issue is that strategies can't traverse from l to p? Strategies typically work by pattern-matching on the first argument of a predicate. If the invariant is `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`, strategies can unfold from l but can't "find" p in the middle of the list.

Actually, wait. I'm not sure strategies work that way. Let me think about what unfolding means.

When QCP is at the body `p = p->next;` with pre-state `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` and p != 0:
- QCP needs to read `p->next`
- QCP tries to unfold `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` to get `data_at(l->next, ...)`
- But l != p (after the first iteration), so `data_at(l->next, ...)` is NOT `data_at(p->next, ...)`
- QCP needs to keep unfolding until it reaches p

This might be beyond what the strategies can do. Strategies handle one-step unfoldings, not recursive traversal.

So the invariant MUST have p as the first argument of some shape predicate to enable direct unfolding at p.

The question is: how to express `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` where `prev` is p->prev, without using `p->prev` in a way that causes dup_data_at errors?

Actually, wait. `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` - let me think about what QCP does with this.

In QCP's internal representation, `dlistrep_shape` is defined as:
```
dlistrep_shape(l, p) = (l == 0 && emp) || exists t, data_at(field_addr(l, next), struct list*, t) * data_at(field_addr(l, prev), struct list*, p) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)
```

`dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` with a concrete p ≠ 0 would unfold to:
```
exists t: data_at(field_addr(p, next), struct list*, t) * data_at(field_addr(p, prev), struct list*, p->prev) * dlistrep_shape(t, p)
```

Where `p->prev` is the VALUE of the prev field. But QCP needs to read `p->prev` to get this value, which requires `data_at(field_addr(p, prev), ...)`. But that's the same field that `data_at(field_addr(p, prev), struct list*, p->prev)` claims!

THIS is the dup_data_at error. The `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` parameter `p->prev` requires reading `p->prev`, which is the same field that the predicate would provide when unfolded. So QCP is in a circular dependency: it needs the prev field to parameterize the predicate, but the predicate provides the prev field.

So `dlistrep_shape(p, p->prev)` doesn't work because the parameter `p->prev` is a FIELD READ, not a simple expression.

This is a crucial insight. Field access expressions in predicate parameters cause issues because they require reading from the heap.

So I need to NOT use `p->prev` and instead use a simple variable or value.

What if I use `dlistrep_shape(p, head)`? At entry (p = l), this is `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` which matches the precondition. But after p advances, `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` claims a list starting at p with prev = head, which is WRONG because p's prev is not head (unless p = l).

Hmm, but maybe this doesn't matter for verification? The postcondition only needs `dlistrep_shape(__return, head)` which is `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`. If the invariant claims `dlistrep_shape(l, head) * dlistrep_shape(p, head)`, that's double-counting.

What if I only have `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` WITHOUT `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`? The invariant would need to cover the entire heap, which is the list from l. `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` only covers from p onward, not from l to p.

Unless... `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` is the same list as `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` when l = p? No, they're different when l ≠ p.

Hmm. What about `dllseg_shape(l, head, p, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, head)`?

When p = l (entry):
- `dllseg_shape(l, head, l, l)` = `dllseg_shape(l, head, l, l)`. Base case: x==y (l==l) requires xp==yp (head==l). This is only true if head == l. Otherwise, it's the non-empty case. Hmm.

When p ≠ l (after some iterations):
- `dllseg_shape(l, head, p, p)` doesn't make sense because the last param (y) should be the endpoint, and the segment goes from x to y (y not included). If y = p and yp = p, the segment stops at p with p->next = p (self-loop)? That's wrong.

OK, this formulation is incorrect.

Let me think more carefully. The `dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y)` means:
- Segment starts at x
- x->prev = xp
- Segment ends at yp (last node inside the segment)
- yp->next = y (y is the endpoint marker, NOT inside the segment)

So `dllseg_shape(l, head, p_prev, p)` means:
- List from l to p (p not included)
- l->prev = head
- Last node in segment = p_prev
- p_prev->next = p

And `dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` means:
- List starting at p
- p->prev = p_prev

Together they partition the list at p. The cut is clean: p_prev is the node before p.

Now, if I use a free variable `q` instead of `p->prev`:

```
dllseg_shape(l, head, q, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, q)
```

Where `q` is the value of `p->prev`. QCP would existentially quantify `q`. The issue is whether QCP can infer that `q = p->prev`.

In the verification, when the loop starts:
- p = l, so `dllseg_shape(l, head, q, l) * dlistrep_shape(l, q)` 
- `dlistrep_shape(l, q)` with a free `q` - this means a list from l where l->prev = q
- But from the precondition, we know l->prev = head, so q must be head
- `dllseg_shape(l, head, q, l)` - when y = x (l = l), the base case requires xp = yp, i.e., head = q. So if head = q, this = emp. Otherwise it's non-empty and tries to follow next pointers from l, which would fail.

Hmm, but q IS free. QCP would need to figure out that q = head for the entrance check to work. This might be beyond what auto strategies can handle.

After the first iteration (body `p = p->next`):
- New p = old_p->next
- We know old_p->next = some value, and old_p->prev = q
- The invariant needs to hold with new p

This is getting very complex. Let me step back and try a completely practical approach.

Let me try with a simpler structure. The invariant that should work:

dlistrep_shape(l, head) * dlistrep_shape(p, p)
```

Nope, that's two lists.

I can't use &p.

OK, let me try yet another practical approach. The issue is that `has_permission(&p)` segfaults with annotations. What if I use the `p == p` trick to keep p in scope, but ALSO add a PROP constraint that gives strategies what they need?

The strategies can't prove that p->next is readable from `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`.

Which is essentially saying there exists some list that connects l to p, and some list from p to the end.

This is `data_at(&(p->prev), ...)`. The `&` is the address-of operator on the field, which gives us `field_addr(p, prev)`. QCP created a `data_at(field_addr(p, prev), ...)` entity, which duplicates with the one inside `dlistrep_shape`.

Here, `p_287_value` is the existential from p. `l_286_value` is the existential from l. The right side needs `data_at(field_addr(p_287_value, next), struct list*, v)`.

QCP has `dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)` on the left. If `l_286_value == p_287_value`, this would unfold to `data_at(field_addr(l_286_value, next), ...)` = `data_at(field_addr(p_287_value, next), ...)`.

1. Unfold `dlistrep_shape(l_286_value, head)` to get `data_at(l_286_value->next, t) * data_at(l_286_value->prev, head) * dlistrep_shape(t, l_286_value)`

Right side: `data_at(p_287_value->next, v)`

If `t == p_287_value`, then the left has `dlistrep_shape(p_287_value, l_286_value)`, and unfolding gives `data_at(p_287_value->next, ...)`. But QCP needs `t == p_287_value`, which means `l_286_value->next == p_287_value`.

This is fundamentally a strategy limitation. The strategies are designed to unfold predicates, not to match existential variables to program variables.

Now I see the strategies. Let me analyze what rules apply:

**Rule 5**: Unfolds `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` when `p != NULL` into:
- `data_at(p->next, y)` 
- `data_at(p->prev, prev)`
- `dlistrep_shape(y, p)`

**Rule 6**: Same as Rule 5 but for right side.

**Rule 7**: Combines `dllseg_shape(p, q, r, s)` on left with `dlistrep_shape(p, x)` on right into `dlistrep_shape(s, r)` on right. (Used when the left has a segment and right has the start of that segment)

**Rule 8**: When left has `dlistrep_shape(p, x)` and right has `dlistrep_shape(p, y)` (same p), replaces both with `x == y`.

**Rule 12**: Combines `dllseg_shape(p, q, r, p)` (segment ending at p) + `dlistrep_shape(p, x)` on right into `q == r && x == q`.

The crucial insight from Rule 5: **To unfold dlistrep_shape, QCP needs `p != 0` in the PROP**. This is required!

So when we have the invariant `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` and the loop is `while (p) { p = p->next; }`:
1. At the loop body, QCP tries to prove `p->next` is readable
2. It needs to unfold dlistrep_shape to get `data_at(p->next, ...)`

4. We have `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` not `dlistrep_shape(p, ...)`, so Rule 5 doesn't apply to p!

left : dlistrep_shape(?p : Z, ?prev) at 0

```

When unfolding `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` (where prev is the second parameter), it:
1. Erases dlistrep_shape(p, prev) from left
2. Adds existential y
3. Adds data_at(p->next, y) to left
4. Adds data_at(p->prev, prev) to left
5. Adds dlistrep_shape(y, p) to left

   - Pattern: `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` on left AND `(p != NULL || NULL != p)` in PROP
   - This matches the pattern of the goal (data_at(p->next, ...) on right)

4. Now left has `data_at(p->next, y)`, and right needs `data_at(p->next, v)` → solved by matching y = v

For preservation, we need: The invariant after body execution should still hold. QCP would:

test_report() {
  local label="$1"
  local inv="$2"
  
  python3 -c "
with open('$SKILL_DIR/dataset_qcp_infill/System/Zephyr/changed_codes/dlist/sys_dlist_t/iterator/iter.c', 'r') as f:

```

This is the preservation check. The invariant `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, head)` was true BEFORE the body. After body execution, `p = p->next`:
1. Read p->next = y, p becomes y
2. `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` was unfolded to get p->next → `data_at(p->next, y) * data_at(p->prev, head) * dlistrep_shape(y, p)`

Left side (actual state):
- `data_at(p->next, y)` 
- `dlistrep_shape(y, p)`
- `data_at(p->prev, head)`
- `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p)`

- `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, y)`
- `dlistrep_shape(y, head)`

I have some observations:
1. Left has `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` - right needs `dlistrep_shape(y, head)` - the prev differs (p vs head)!
2. Left has `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p) * data_at(p->next, y)` - needs to form `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, y)`
3. Left has `data_at(p->prev, head)` - this is from the unfolded dlistrep_shape

For issue 1: This is the fundamental problem. `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` has prev=p, but the invariant wants `dlistrep_shape(y, head)` with prev=head. These are incompatible when p ≠ head (which is generally true after the first iteration).

Actually, looking at this from the unfolding perspective:
- `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, y)` unfolds at the START (l), not at the end (p).

This works for the first iteration!

- Before body: invariant `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`, p = t, p ≠ 0

Looking at the strategies: Rule 5 unfolds `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` when `p ≠ 0`. After the first unfold, we have `dlistrep_shape(t, l)`. If t = p, then this is `dlistrep_shape(p, l)`. But Rule 5 checks for `p ≠ 0`, and during the SECOND iteration, the PROP has `p ≠ 0` (from the loop condition). Now p = t, so Rule 5 can unfold `dlistrep_shape(t, l)` = `dlistrep_shape(p, l)` to get `data_at(p->next, ...)`.

Wait, but the variable `p` and the existential `t` might not be equal in the QCp internal representation. Let me check.

During the second iteration:
- Invariant: `dlistrep_shape(l_286, head)` 
- p = p_287, and p_287 = t (from the first iteration)

After the first iteration, the state has:
- `data_at(l_286->next, t) * data_at(l_286->prev, head) * dlistrep_shape(t, l_286)`
- p = t (p_287 = t)

For the second iteration:
- Need: `data_at(field_addr(p_287, next), ...)` = `data_at(field_addr(t, next), ...)`
- Left has: `data_at(l_286->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, l_286)` (and more)
- dlistrep_shape(t, l_286) can be unfolded when t ≠ 0 in PROP → gives `data_at(t->next, u)`
- This matches `data_at(field_addr(p_287, next), ...)` since p_287 = t

So the key is: can the strategy match `p_287` (a program variable) with `t` (an existential) in the constraint?

In the entailment, the left side has:
```
exists t_290, ..., dlistrep_shape(t_290, l_286)
```
And the right side needs:
```
data_at(&(p_287->next), ...)
```

The strategy Rule 5 looks for `dlistrep_shape(?p, ?prev)` where `?p` matches `p_287`. But the left side has `dlistrep_shape(t_290, l_286)`, not `dlistrep_shape(p_287, l_286)`. So Rule 5 can't match because `t_290 ≠ p_287` (different existential).

Only if `t_290 = p_287` would the match work. But they are different variables.

This is the fundamental issue: QCP creates a new existential `t_290` for the unfolded node, but the program variable `p_287` holds the SAME VALUE. The strategies don't know that `t_290 = p_287`.

In the first iteration, this works because `p_287 = l_286` (p = l), so unfolding `dlistrep_shape(l_286, head)` gives `data_at(l_286->next, t_290)`, and since `p_287 = l_286`, `data_at(l_286->next, t_290)` = `data_at(p_287->next, t_290)`. The strategy matches because `?p` in Rule 5 is bound to `l_286` which matches `p_287`.

In the second iteration, `p_287 ≠ l_286`, so there's no direct match.

So the issue is: QCP needs to track the EQUALITY between the existential `t_290` and the program variable `p_287`.

For the first iteration, this equality is implicit (both p and l point to the same node). But after `p = p->next`, p gets a NEW value that's different from l.

The preservation check:
After body execution `p = p->next`, QCP assigns p a new value. At this point, the state has the unfolded `dlistrep_shape` predicates, and p is bound to a specific value.

For the second iteration, the state might have:
```
data_at(l_286->next, p_287) * dlistrep_shape(p_287, l_286) * data_at(l_286->prev, head)
```
Here, the existential `t_290` was bound to `p_287` by the assignment `p = t_290`. So now `p_287 = t_290`.

When the second iteration's body executes, the state should have `dlistrep_shape(p_287, l_286)` already, and Rule 5 can unfold it (with PROP `p_287 ≠ 0`). This gives `data_at(p_287->next, u)` ✓.

So the preservation works! Let me re-check: why did the first run with just `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` fail?

Oh wait, the first run with `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` (no p in scope at all) failed at the FIRST while condition evaluation because p was dropped from the scope. The error was "cannot find the program variable p".

So the fix is: use `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` as the invariant AND keep p in scope somehow.

The problem has always been: how to keep p in scope? And the answer from the annotation-free version is: `has_permission(&p)`. But it segfaults with annotations.

After body: state has both unfold levels:

Step 2: `data_at(l_286->next, t) * data_at(l_286->prev, head) * dlistrep_shape(t, l_286)` → `dlistrep_shape(l_286, head)` ✓

Rule 8:
```
left : dlistrep_shape(?p : Z, ?x) at 0
right : dlistrep_shape(p, ?y) at 1
action : left_erase(0);
         right_erase(1);
         right_add(x == y);
```

This rule is for when the same first arg is on left and right. But here we have different prev args.

Actually, the issue is that entail_wit_2 needs to prove:
```

```

The left has `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` and the right needs `dlistrep_shape(y, head)`. Rule 8:
- Left matching `?p` = y, `?x` = p
- Right matching `p` = y, `?y` = head
- Action: erase both, add `p == head` as PROP
- New goal: `p == head && ... |-- ...`

But `p == head` is FALSE when p = l->next and head = l->prev (generally p ≠ head). So the entailment fails.

The rule essentially leaves the goal `p == head` to be proven (or refuted). Since p ≠ head, the goal is false, and the proof fails.

Bash: SKILL_DIR=/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference
RESULT_DIR=/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260522_202658/System/Zephyr/changed_codes/dlist/sys_dlist_t/iterator

test_report() {
  local label="$1"
  local inv="$2"
  python3 -c "
with open('$SKILL_DIR/dataset_qcp_infill/System/Zephyr/changed_codes/dlist/sys_dlist_t/iterator/iter.c', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()
content = content.replace('/* INFILL */', '$inv')
with open('$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_inv.c', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)
"
  rm -f "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_goal.v" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_auto.v" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_goal_check.v" 2>/dev/null
  "$SKILL_DIR/scripts/symexec" \
    --goal-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_goal.v" \
    --proof-auto-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_auto.v" \
    --proof-manual-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" \
    --coq-logic-path=SimpleC.EE.infra.dll_nodata_shape_lib \
    -slp "$SKILL_DIR/scripts/infra/" SimpleC.EE.infra \
    --input-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_inv.c" \
    --basic-assertion --primary-assertion \
    > "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>&1
  local ec=$?
  local success=$(grep -c "Successfully finished" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  local admitted=999
  if [ -f "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" ]; then
    admitted=$(grep -c "Admitted\." "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  fi
  local fatal=$(grep -c "fatal error" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  local seg=$(grep -c "Segmentation" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  echo "$label -> ec=$ec Success=$success Admitted=$admitted Fatal=$fatal Seg=$seg"
}

# More creative approaches

test_report "dlistrep(l,head)*dlistrep(p,0)" '/*@ Inv Assert dlistrep_shape(l, head) * dlistrep_shape(p, 0) */'
test_report "dlistrep(p,l)" '/*@ Inv Assert dlistrep_shape(p, l) */'

For the right side `dlistrep_shape(y, head)`, we need to match with `dlistrep_shape(y, p)` on the left. The only difference is `p` vs `head`.

If I could prove `p == head`, the goal would be solved. But `p = head` is generally false.

However! What if the actual data structure has `head = p`? For a non-empty list at entry, l->prev = head. At the first iteration, p = l and head = l->prev. If the list has head = 0 (which is the common case for the head of a list), then head = 0. And p = l ≠ 0 (for non-empty list). So `p ≠ head`.

But after the first iteration, what's the relationship? p = l->next, and `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` was unfolded from `dlistrep_shape(l, head)`. The unfold gave `data_at(p->prev, head)`, which means p->prev = head.

But wait, in the ACTUAL program, p->prev = l (since p = l->next). So `data_at(p->prev, head)` from the unfold means the VALUE of p->prev is `head`. But head is l->prev. And l->prev = head. So p->prev = l, not head.

This means the invariant `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` is introducing INCORRECT information about the prev value. After unfolding, it says `data_at(p->prev, head)`, but the actual state (from the initial `data_at(l->prev, head)` when the list is unfolded properly) would have `data_at(p->prev, l)`. These conflict!

But wait, the entailment is about checking that the INVARIANT is preserved, not that it matches the actual state. The strategy works with the invariant assertions, not the actual program state.

Actually, I think I'm confusing two things:
1. The invariant's consistency with the actual program state
2. The entailment proof

For the entailment `entail_wit_2`, QCP is checking: does the unfolded invariant (after body execution) imply the invariant?

The left side has the POSITION after body execution (p = p->next, and p≠0 condition). The right side is the invariant.

But the left side was constructed by unfolding the ORIGINAL invariant. So it faithfully represents the information in the original invariant, not the actual program state.

If the original invariant `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` says p->prev = head, then unfolding it gives `data_at(p->prev, head)`. This is consistent with the invariant itself, but may NOT be what the actual program state would produce if the program were executed.

Wait, but the preservation entailment should be: from the state AFTER the body executes, can we prove the invariant holds?

The state after the body is obtained by symbolically executing the body on the state where the invariant held. So:

Pre-body state: invariant holds (as an assertion)
After `p = p->next`: the state is derived from the invariant by reading `p->next`

The symbolic execution of `p = p->next` on `dlistrep_shape(p, head)`:
1. Read p: p = p_old
2. Read p->next: unfold `dlistrep_shape(p_old, head)` → `data_at(p_old->next, y) * data_at(p_old->prev, head) * dlistrep_shape(y, p_old)`
3. Assign y to p: p becomes y

After body state: `data_at(p_old->next, y) * data_at(p_old->prev, head) * dlistrep_shape(y, p_old) * dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p_old)` with p = y

```

&& ((&((p) # "list" ->ₛ "next")) # Ptr |-> y)     -- data_at(p->next, y) [but the p here is p_old]

Right side `dlistrep_shape(y, head)`: need list starting at y with prev=head
Left side `dlistrep_shape(y, p)`: list starting at y with prev=p (p is p_old = y's predecessor)

For the proof to work: we need `p = head`. But p (p_old) is l->next or some other node in the list, and head is l->prev. These are different nodes.

So the invariant `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, head)` is fundamentally flawed because the `dlistrep_shape(p, head)` asserts the wrong prev value.

The correct value for `prev` in `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` should be:
- At entry (p=l): prev = head
- After 1st iteration (p=l->next): prev = l
- After 2nd iteration (p=l->next->next): prev = l->next

Which is always: prev = <the node that was at p before the last iteration>.

This is always `dlistrep_shape(p, old_p)` where `old_p` is the value of p before it was updated. Since old_p is lost after the update, the only way to track it is through `dllseg_shape`.

If dllseg_shape has the correct prev value: `dllseg_shape(l, head, prev, p)`, then `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)` would work. At each iteration:
- After body: p = p->next, prev = p_old (the old p)
- `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p_old)` * `dlistrep_shape(p_old, head)` becomes (after unfolding and re-folding):
  - `dllseg_shape(l, head, p_old, p_new)` * `dlistrep_shape(p_new, p_old)`

But for this to work, the yp parameter of dllseg_shape must match the prev of dlistrep_shape. At entry: both use `head`. After extension: one uses `p_old` and the other uses `p_old`.

But yp = `p_old` and prev = `p_old`. They match! The problem in my invariant was that I hard-coded `head` for both, but after extension, they should use `p_old`.

So the correct invariant should have a MATCHING pair of yp and prev that updates DURING each iteration.

The key insight: if I use a FIXED value (like `head`) for both yp and prev, the invariant only works at entry. After p advances, the prev value changes.

To make the invariant work for all iterations, the yp and prev values must somehow UPDATE to reflect the traversal. But since the invariant is a static assertion, this isn't possible without using `p->prev` or free variables.

test_report() {
  local label="$1"
  local inv="$2"
  python3 -c "
with open('$SKILL_DIR/dataset_qcp_infill/System/Zephyr/changed_codes/dlist/sys_dlist_t/iterator/iter.c', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()
content = content.replace('/* INFILL */', '$inv')

    f.write(content)
"
  rm -f "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_goal.v" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_auto.v" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_goal_check.v" 2>/dev/null

    --goal-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_goal.v" \
    --proof-auto-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_auto.v" \
    --proof-manual-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" \
    --coq-logic-path=SimpleC.EE.infra.dll_nodata_shape_lib \
    -slp "$SKILL_DIR/scripts/infra/" SimpleC.EE.infra \
    --input-file="$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_inv.c" \
    --basic-assertion --primary-assertion \
    > "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>&1
  local ec=$?
  local success=$(grep -c "Successfully finished" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  local admitted=999
  if [ -f "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" ]; then
    admitted=$(grep -c "Admitted\." "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_proof_manual.v" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  fi
  local fatal=$(grep -c "fatal error" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  echo "$label -> ec=$ec Success=$success Admitted=$admitted Fatal=$fatal"
}

"

    --basic-assertion --primary-assertion \

  local success=$(grep -c "Successfully finished" "$RESULT_DIR/tmp/iter_verify.log" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)

  echo "$label -> ec=$ec Success=$success Admitted=$admitted Fatal=$fatal Seg=${seg:-none}"
}

## 结果

**文件**: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/dataset_qcp_infill/System/Zephyr/changed_codes/dlist/sys_dlist_t/iterator/iter.c`

**结果**: QCP_VERIFIED: NO (经过最大重试次数后)

**最佳不变量**:
```
/*@ Inv Assert dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, head) */
```

**输出位置**: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260522_202658/System/Zephyr/changed_codes/dlist/sys_dlist_t/iterator/result.txt`

## 已执行步骤

1. **第 1.1 步** -- 使用 `unroll_loop.py` 展开循环（深度 3），带 `--ensure-emp`
2. **第 1.2 步** -- 对展开后的文件运行 `symexec`（成功完成）
3. **第 1.3 步** -- 使用 `qcp_parse_log.py` 解析日志 -> 3 个记录点（RP_pre_loop、RP_iter_2、RP_iter_3）
4. **第 2 步** -- 分析 diff：循环遍历列表而不修改它。框架项是完整的列表。受 `p` 驱动出现展开项。
5. **第 3 步** -- 大量不变量公式测试（>30 种变体）

## 关键发现

**根本问题**: `dlistrep_shape(l, head)` 形式的不变量不能孤立地处理遍历指针 `p`。需要 `dlistrep_shape(p, prev)`，以便策略可以展开 `p` 处的不变量并读取 `p->next`。然而，`prev` 参数（即 `p->prev`）会随着 `p` 的遍历而改变，在没有使用 `p->prev`（导致 `dup_data_at` 错误）或自由变量的情况下，无法静态表达这一点。

**二进制 bug**: 当函数有 `/*@ With ... Require ... Ensure ... */` 注解时，`has_permission(&p)` 会导致段错误。相同的 `has_permission(&p)` 没有注解也能正常工作（例如，已确认的 GlibC 变体）。

**最佳尝试**: `dllseg_shape(l, head, head, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, head)` 在 Coq 证明中留下了 1 个 `Admitted` 目标（`entail_wit_2` -- 不变量保持性）。这是保存检查失败，因为遍历一个节点后，`dlistrep_shape(p, head)` 中第二个参数的值（`head`）不再与 `p` 的前驱节点匹配。

**输出文件**:
- 不变量文件: `$RESULT/tmp/iter_inv.c`
- 工作区文件: `$RESULT/workspace/`（展开后的 .c，symexec 日志，解析后的状态）
- Coq 证明文件: `$RESULT/tmp/`（goal.v，proof_auto.v，proof_manual.v）
